Breakevens seen at sub-WTI $50/b
Devon to spend $5.3 billion in 2027
Two leading US Lower 48 states shale producers, EOG Resources Inc. and Devon Energy Corp., will pursue "singledigit" oil output growth in 2027 while keeping tight reins on capital costs and returning cash to shareholders, they said separately Nov. 6.
"Market fundamentals remain tight," EOG CEO Ezra Yacob said on the company's third-quarter 2026 earnings webcast. "Middle East exports have recovered from lows reached in the spring. However, the recovery has been uneven. While strategic petroleum reserve releases and coordinated refinery measures across the G7 are providing some relief at the margin, they are not a substitute for consistent and reliable supply from the region."
While the expectation will be for prices to remain volatile, supply recoveries remain fragile and are still subject to ongoing disruption risk, Yacob said.

EOG has resources in the Williston, Powder River, Rocky Mountain, Utica, Delaware, Dorado and Eagle Ford basins/plays in the Lower 48.
Devon eyes 2027 output of 525,000 b/d Separately, Devon is targeting 1%-3% growth in crude oil
production in 2027 to 525,000 b/d, with a capital expenditure of $5.3 billion, it said in its third-quarter 2026 earnings release.
"Over the next few years and beyond, we expect to generate low single-digit oil growth with conservative reinvestment rates of 45% to 60% of free cash flow," CEO Clay Gaspar said on the earnings webcast. "This modest oil growth will be driven by the Permian, and where we will direct roughly 75% of our 2027 capital program."
The Permian Basin is a "cornerstone" acreage for Devon and in the current year the company more than doubled its footprint through a series of "intentional moves" that included a merger in February 2026 with fellow producer Coterra Energy with 750,000 net acres of stacked pay, Gaspar said.
"Then just two weeks after we closed, we acquired a once-ina-generation acreage position via the federal lease sale, which is completely untouched from grass to granite and it boasts some of the highest returns in the industry," he said.

For its part, Devon continues to expand its inventory in the Permian Basin by delineating new landing zones across the company's expansive multi-target position, adding locations organically through the application of technology, and executing a ground-game acreage leasing and trades, as well as ongoing exploration with downhole potential, Gaspar said.
The company's operational activity in the third quarter averaged 32 drilling rigs and eight completion crews, resulting in 124 net operated wells being placed online, with an average lateral length of 11,400 feet, the release said, adding that cash costs averaged $12.89/barrels of oil equivalent.

Plains signs decade-long Permian deal, adding dedicated acreage
Signs 10-year deal adding 270,000 acres
Expects 200,000 b/d Permian growth by 2026
Plains All American has signed a 10-year agreement with a large integrated producer to expand its Permian Midland gathering system. The deal adds 270,000 dedicated acres and brings Plains' total Permian dedicated acreage to more than 5.3 million, CEO Wilfred Chiang said on the company's third-quarter earnings call Nov. 6.
The acreage is existing production, so the deal will not lift earnings until the connection is built. Chief Commercial Officer Jeremy Gobel said the contribution should come in "late 2027, early 2028," and Plains will give more guidance on capital and timing next year.
"It's not a ramp to production. It's just a matter of timing for connection," Gobel said in response to an analyst. "We're excited about the opportunity to partner with a top-tier producer."
The agreement gives more growth opportunities to the Pop JV, said Chiang, who declined to specify which producer the company was partnering with.
CFO Al Swanson said the company is narrowing its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to plus or minus $50 million around a midpoint of $2.88 billion. "Based on [the] prevailing macro environment, we would expect to be at, or slightly above the midpoint of the range for the year," he said.
Plains now reports one consolidated segment, which Swanson said matches the May closing of the sale of its Canadian NGL business. Chiang said the sale completed Plains' shift to a "premier crude oil midstream company."
Permian outlook Chiang said Plains expects Permian production growth toward
the high end of its previous range, at about 200,000 b/d on a 2026 exit-to-exit basis. He cited better natural gas egress, higher rig counts over the past six months, continued development of various formations, and advances in drilling efficiency and enhanced recovery techniques.

He pointed to 30 rigs added this year, better efficiency and the start of added completion crews. Pipelines to the coast are "close to 90% full now," and the basin will grow by that amount for several more years. The executive said the analyst's timing for additional brownfield expansions, "in that '28 to '29 time period," is appropriate.
"There's no need for a greenfield, large pipeline at this time based on the ratable growth that we see," he added.
Plains closed the Silver Creek acquisition on Oct. 1 and renamed it Silver Spur. Chiang said the system moves about 125,000 b/d and has 915,000 dedicated acres under long-term contracts. Integrating it with Plains' network gives customers long-haul connectivity to Cushing, he said.
He added that Plains has captured $50 million of synergies from the Capistry pipeline acquisition. It remains on track for $50 million of efficiencies across the organization by year-end 2026.
-- Binish Azhar

REFINERY NEWS: Turkey's Tupras expects sharply higher margins for 2026
Refineries: Izmit, Izmir, Kirikkale and Batman, Turkey

with Iran yet," said Global Risk Management Chief Analyst Arne Lohmann Rasmussen.
Ship traffic through the Strait of Hormuz ticked up to 26 transits Nov. 5 compared with 21 the day prior, according to the latest report from S&P Global Commodities at Sea, even as government and security sources continued to report ship attacks in the region.
On Nov. 6, the UK Maritime Trade Operations Centre reported an explosion on a tanker 16 nautical miles east of Khasab, Oman, when the ship exited Hormuz. The British government agency verified 11 incidents between Oct. 29 and Nov. 4, compared with three in the prior week.
NYMEX December RBOB climbed 1.33 cents/gallon to $2.7767/ gal and December ULSD declined 2.02 cents/gal to $4.3317/b.
While crude futures remained below the $100/b threshold, concerns of returning hostilities between the US and Iran kept a firm floor beneath pricing, analysts said.
Global Risk Management wrote in a morning note Nov. 6 that there was a 45% chance of escalation in the conflict, a 30% chance of a deal that leads to the reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a 25% chance of the status quo with diplomatic talks returning without a breakthrough.
"Although political pressure on Trump has increased since the election, his weakened position could also make reaching an agreement harder. Iran may demand greater concessions than

Owner: Tupras
Overall capacity: 602,000 b/d Turkey's main refiner, Tupras, expects its 2026 refining margin
to average $20-$22/barrel, the company announced in its thirdquarter results Nov. 6.
This is sharply up from the $13-$15/barrel Tupras had predicted in its H1 results and more than three times the $6-$7/b the company announced in its Q1 results
The company cited ongoing geopolitical tensions in the Middle East, combined with supply and logistics risks in the Strait of Hormuz and the Red Sea, which tightened market conditions and bolstered product margins throughout the third quarter.
The company added that refinery outages in the Middle East, coupled with regional supply risks and export restrictions imposed by Russia, had caused diesel margins to reach historic highs and remain high for the duration of the quarter.
In addition, Tupras said that jet fuel margins had remained high due to tight conditions in the middle distillate market and strong demand in the aviation sector, while gasoline margins also remained high due to strong demand, low inventory levels following the summer season and the geopolitical situation, which caused refineries to prioritize diesel production.
Tupras said its production expectations for its four refineries had not changed since Q1, with the Turkish market still showing

Platts Oilgram Price Report

November 6, 2026

year-over-year growth. The company said it expected 2026 output of about 29 million
mt, at a capacity utilization rate of 95%-100% and sales of about 30 million mt.
Tupras added that it plans to invest about $700 million during 2026, unchanged from its plans announced in Q1.
Tupras said that its refinery output in the first nine months of 2026 totaled 21.253 million mt, up 5.5% from the first nine months of 2025.
Third-quarter output totaled 7.62 million mt, up 11.1% from Q2 and up 5.8% from Q3 2025, the data showed.
The company didn't give a reason for the increase in output but noted that data from Turkey's energy market regulatory authority EPDK showed that over the first eight months of 2026, Turkey's gasoline demand was 4.1 million mt, up 10.2% compared to the same period in 2025 and diesel demand rose 1.2% to 17.7 million mt.
Tupras said that its crude throughput over the nine-month period was 22.228 million mt, up 5.5% from the same period in 2025.
Third-quarter throughput was 7.966 million mt, up 11.0% on Q2 and up 5.9% on Q3 2025, the data showed.
Of the 21.253 million mt produced, 82.7% was light products, down from 83% in H1 but up from 82% reported over the first nine months of 2025.
Tupras said that the average API of crudes processed over the nine-month period was 32.2, down from the 32.3 reported in H1 and above the API over the first nine months of 2025. Capacity utilization over the nine months was 98.8%, up from 95.1% in H1 and from the 93.6% reported for the first nine months of 2025 when the company's Izmir refinery was undergoing maintenance.
Tupras said that of its 21.253 million mt output over the first nine months of 2026, 7.823 million mt was diesel, up 6% from the same period in 2025, 4.866 million mt was gasoline, up 7.1% year over year and 3.936 million mt was jet, up 5.9% year over year.
LPG output increased by 11.4% year over year at 715,000 mt, and bitumen output rose 5.5% at 2.219 million mt.
Output of fuel oil fell 11.7% year over year at 687,000 mt, while naphtha output was 20,000 mt, up from just 1,000 mt a year earlier.
Tupras said its overall sales for the first nine months of 2026 were 24.025 million mt, up 9.6% year over year.
Domestic sales were 19.572 million mt, up 12.3% year over year, with Q3 domestic sales of 7.896 million mt, up 29% from Q2 and up 13.5% year over year.
There were across-the-board rises in domestic sales of all products.
Export sales for the nine months totaled 4.453 million mt, down 0.8% year over year, with Q3 exports of 877,000 mt, down 8.4% year over year and down 49.7% on Q2, the data showed.
Exports of LPG, gasoline, diesel and fuel oils were all sharply down, reflecting global shortages with exports of jet, bitumen and of "other" non-transport fuels sharply up, reflecting Turkey's excess capacity in those products.

Exports of diesel fell 43.6%, a sharp drop believed to reflect the halting of exports by Russia, which has been Turkey's largest supplier of diesel imports, in turn believed to be due to attacks on Russian refineries causing shortages in Russia's domestic market.
-- David O'Byrne
REFINERY NEWS: Montana Renewables cuts MaxSAF costs by tying in existing refinery equipment
Refinery: Great Falls, Montana
Owner: Calumet
Overall capacity: 12,000 b/d petroleum fuels and 12,000 b/d renewables
Units affected: N/A
Duration: 2026-2028
Calumet Inc. has begun tying in leased equipment from its legacy refinery into its Montana Renewables facility, a move that will accelerate sustainable aviation fuel production expansion and lift throughput, CEO Todd Borgmann said on Nov. 6.
"This began this week as planned, and we're expecting to be back online this month to increase rates, improve our naphtha yields, and ramp up SAF production with our new SAF polishing process," he said on the third-quarter results call.
Montana Renewables' SAF polishing service will allow it to "process SAF at a lower severity than the industry standard cracking service" so the company can produce SAF while minimizing volume loss and naphtha production.
"This underpins our path to reach 80 million annual gallon SAF run rate by year end, 120 million by spring, 120 to 150 million by end of '27, and ultimately 200 million gallons in 2028," he said.
Montana Renewables will also capture and sell RIN-generating NGLs, reduce water usage, and "creep up throughput to 17,000 b/d throughout the project," Borgmann said, which meets Calumets 2028 total renewable fuel production target.
Borgmann said the work includes tying in the hydrotreater, hydrogen plant and naphtha splitter, which under terms of its Nov. 5 announcement are leased to Montana Renewables (MRL) from Calumet Montana Refining (CMR) after a new lease agreement with partner Warburg Pincus unlocked critical assets at a fraction of the originally planned capital cost
Borgmann said the timing of the work allowed the refinery "captured peak asphalt season and the elevated margins ahead the reconfiguration."
The structure of the new agreement, developed along the US Department of Energy under an earlier amended loan, is expected to save roughly $1 billion of capital compared to the original MaxOut expansion plan.
The total cost of the revised six-step MaxSAF project is $137 million, or less than 15% of the originally planned capital outlay, Borgmann said.
"An additional 5 million cubic feet per day of hydrogen capacity will also increase throughput by 1,000 b/d immediately,"

Phillips 66 reports coker process upset at Sweeny refinery
Refinery: Sweeny, Texas
Owner: Phillips 66
Overall capacity: 277,000 b/d
Units affected: Coker
Duration: Nov. 6, 7:11 am-8:58 am CT
Phillips 66 Company reported an emissions event at its Sweeny, Texas, refinery on Nov. 6, according to a Nov. 6 filing with the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality (TCEQ).
"The refinery experienced a process upset. Operations worked to stabilize the units and return them to normal operating conditions," the TCEQ filing said.
According to the TCEQ filing, the incident was discovered at 7:11 am CST on Nov. 6 and ended at 8:58 am CST the same day. The event involved Unit 26.2, identified as the Hydrogen Purification Unit, and the emission point was the Coker Flare.
Prices of USGC pipeline ULSD on Nov. 6 dropped back into negative territory, holding a 4-cent/gal discount to NYMEX ULSD futures amid cycle changes, according to assessments from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy. On Nov. 5, Platts had assessed USGC ULSD at a 1.75-cent/gal premium to the screen.

REFINERY NEWS: Flint Hills begins maintenance at Corpus plant; ULSD prices flip to positive
Refinery: Corpus Christi, Texas
Owner: Flint Hills
Overall capacity: 357,000 b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Nov. 6-13
Flint Hills is beginning planned maintenance at the West Plant of its Corpus Christi, Texas, refining, according to the company's Nov. 6 notification to local regulators.
"Operations at Flint Hills Resources West Plant is conducting planned maintenance work and planned flaring is necessary for the next seven days. At this time, no community action is

required," stated the notification made with the Corpus Christi Reverse Alert system.
No further information was provided regarding the scope of the maintenance, which follows a spate of unplanned filings with regulators. A company spokesperson was not immediately available for comment.
On Nov. 2, Flint Hills reported a diesel hydrotreater upset at the larger West plant, while on Oct. 27, the company reported flaring at both the East and West plants.
Prices of US Gulf Coast pipeline ULSD flipped to the positive, holding a 1.75 cents/gal premium to NYMEX ULSD futures on Nov. 5, according to Platts assessments. On Nov. 4, Platts assessed USGC ULSD at a 3.25 cents/gal discount to the screen. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

REFINERY NEWS: Japan's Taiyo Oil to shut Shikoku CDUs for planned maintenance in Jan
Refinery: Ehime in western Japan
Owner: Taiyo Oil
Overall capacity: 138,000 b/d
Units affected: 106,000 b/d for No. 1 CDU, 32,000 b/d for No. 2 CDU
Duration: early Jan to mid-March 2027 for both CDUs
Japanese refiner Taiyo Oil will shut its 106,000 b/d No. 1 crude distillation unit and 32,000 b/d No. 2 CDU at its sole Shikoku refinery in Ehime, western Japan, for planned maintenance in early January 2027, with both units set to restart in mid-March 2027, a company spokesperson told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on Nov. 6.
The official said the refiner will systematically supply oil products from its stocks during the maintenance period, though the supply may not go as planned amid the current tough conditions for procuring crude oil.
Platts assessed the average prices of gasoline, kerosene and gasoil at ¥96,100/kiloliter, ¥114,800/kl and ¥100,600/kl, respectively, across the Chiba, Kanagawa, Chukyo and Hanshin regions Nov. 6.

REFINERY NEWS: Hungary's MOL Q3 throughput down 1.4% on year
Refinery: Duna (Danube) in Szazhalombatta, near Budapest, Hungary
Owner: MOL Group
Overall capacity: 165,000 b/d (8.1 million mt/year)
Refinery: Slovnaft, Bratislava, Slovakia
Owner: MOL Group's Slovnaft unit
Capacity: 122,000 b/d
Refinery: Rijeka, Croatia
Owner: INA
Overall capacity: 90,000 b/d
Units affected: Various
Duration: Q3 2026
Notes: MOL Group's refinery throughput fell by 1.4% year on year in the third quarter of 2026 to 4.15 million metric tons, as the company's flagship Danube refinery in Hungary still operated at reduced capacity following an October 2025 fire at one of its distillation units, MOL said in its Q3 earnings presentation on Nov. 6.
The drop in throughput was moderated by additional diesel

output from Rijeka (back online in September after a lengthy delayed-coker construction project) and a low base affected by regular maintenance in Q3 last year, according to MOL.
In the first nine months of 2026, throughput was still down 14% year on year at 11.31 million mt. That gap could close in Q4 as the AV3 unit affected by the fire was restarted in October and reached full capacity by the end of the month, MOL said earlier.
In terms of MOL's Q3 refinery production, diesel output increased by 1.8% (and its share within total output to 43.3%), but gasoline output fell by 4.7% and its share within the total yield shrank to 21.3%. Among other notable changes, the combined share of fuel oil and bitumen shrank from 7.1% to 5.3% of total production.
MOL's external sales of its refined products were unchanged on the year at 5.57 million mt, although this was also up 1.4% to 5.36 million mt when excluding petrochemical products.
MOL said that central European fuel demand was unchanged year on year in Q3. Despite this, the company saw an identical 4.4% year on year rise in both diesel and gasoline sales.
To meet demand amid reduced capacity at Danube, MOL sold 1.97 million mt of products purchased from third parties, 18% more than a year earlier, while sales of its own output were down 6% to 3.39 million mt.
MOL's Brent-based group-wide refinery margin surged in Q3, rising almost fourfold from a year earlier and nearly doubling from Q2 to $37.5/barrel, which MOL attributed to tightening global supply and logistics bottlenecks. Within the total, MOL reported a combined $38.8/b margin at the Danube and Bratislava refineries.
MOL said it saw its refinery margins correct downward to a still-high $23.8/b in October, including a combined $24.8/b at Danube and Bratislava.
Source: MOL -- Balazs Szladek
REFINERY NEWS: Delek US sees strong diesel demand as it discusses DPA for Tyler plant
Refinery: Big Spring, Texas; Tyler, Texas; Krotz Spring, Louisiana; and El Dorado, Arkansas
Owner: Delek US
Overall capacity: 73,000 b/d; 74,000 b/d; 80,000 b/d; and 83,000 b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Q4 2026
Delek US sees strong diesel and jet demand into winter as US and global distillate inventories are low and the economy is growing, as the company discusses the Defense Production Act (DPA) with the administration, CEO Avigal Soreq said on Nov. 6.
"Bottom line on diesel, you have seen inventories low in the US, and probably across the globe. And it's something that is going to take a second to fix," he said on Delek US' third quarter results call.

November 6, 2026

"We've seen the economy on Main Street is doing pretty well....and that requires diesel and a lot of diesel,. And we are up to the winter. And that is another tailwind for diesel prices," he added.
The Nov. 6 jobs report showed US employers added 165,000 jobs in October while other government data showed the real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized rate in the third quarter, signaling solid labor market and economic momentum.
On operations, all four refineries ran reliably in the third quarter of 2026, with total throughput of 316,291 b/d with a refining production margin of $38.56/b. This compares to Q3 2025 throughput of 314,161 b/d, with a refining production margin of $9.59/b. In the fourth quarter, Delek US expects system throughput to range between 288,000 b/d-308,000 b/d:
During Q3 2026, Delek US' Big Spring, Texas, plant throughput averaged 74,706 b/d, with a refining production margin of $41.07/b, compared with the Q3 2025 throughput of 70,128 and a refining production margin of $10.99/b. Q3 2026 capture rate was 68.1% with a distillate yield of 39%. In Q4, Delek US expects Big Spring throughput of 68,000 b/d-73,000 b/d.
Delek's US Tyler, Texas, refinery in Q3 2026 averaged 74,817 b/d throughput with a refining production margin of $42.49/b, compared with the Q3 2025 throughput of 76,092 b/d and a refining margin of $11.32/b. Q3 2026 capture rate was 66.9% with a distillate yield of 46%. In Q4, Delek US expects Tyler throughput to range between 75,000 b/d-80,000 b/d.
At the El Dorado, Arkansas, plant, Q3 2026 refinery throughput averaged 80,690 b/d, with a refining production margin of $33.60/b, compared with the Q3 2025 throughput of 82,864 b/d and a refining production margin of $7.43/b. Q3 2026 capture rate was 52.9% with a distillate yield of 2%. In Q4, Delek US expects El Dorado throughput to range between 68,000 b/d -72,000 b/d.
In Louisiana, Delek US' Krotz Spring Q3 2026 refinery throughput averaged 86,078 b/d, with a refining production margin of $37.60/b, compared with Q3 2025 throughput of 85,077 b/d and a refining production margin of $9.01/b. Q3 capture rate was 65.8% with a distillate yield of 47%. In Q4, Delek US expects Krotz Springs throughput to range from 78,000 b/d-83,000 b/d.
With the 2026 turnaround program done, Soreq said the system is "well positioned" for the fourth quarter, adding Delek US will give 2027 turnaround guidance on its next earnings call.
Soreq confirmed that Delek US had been talking to the Trump Administration about getting some assistance through the Defense Production Act (DPA) for its Tyler facility, but stressed that the refinery would remain rated at 75,000 b/d, the cut-off for Small Refinery Exemption (SRE) eligibility.
The DPA gives the federal government authority to prioritize contracts and mobilize resources to support national defense and emergency needs.
"We are talking about shifting between different kind of products," he said.
"We're obviously very thankful for the Administration for helping refineries in the US," he added.
-- Janet Mcgurty

REFINERY NEWS: Citgo Corpus Christi FCCU shuts after communication issue
Refinery: Corpus Christi, Texas
Owner: Citgo
Overall capacity: 165,000 b/d
Units affected: FCCU
Duration: Nov. 5 at 3:13 pm CT
Citgo's No. 2 fluid catalytic cracking unit at its Corpus Christi, Texas, refinery experienced an unplanned shutdown Nov. 5 following communication issues, according to a filing with the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality on Nov. 6.
The FCCU at the refinery's East Plant shut suddenly at 3:13 pm CT Nov. 5, resulting in elevated carbon monoxide emissions and excess opacity from the unit's regenerator and electrostatic precipitator, or ESP, the filing showed.
"Citgo operations personnel responded quickly and safely to minimize emissions and stabilize refinery process units during the unplanned shutdown and controlled startup," the company said in the filing.
The filing lists the event as ending at 3:13 pm Nov. 6, indicating the refinery had moved through a controlled start-up following the shutdown.
A company spokesperson was not immediately available for comment.
-- Janet Mcgurty

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye

Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts launches LR1 Persian Gulf-Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment Nov 2, 2026
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has launched a new Long Range I {LR1} Persian Gulf to Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
This decision follows extensive industry consultations and analysis of spot fixtures and market data available since 2019, as well as direct feedback from market participants.
An analysis of spot fixtures on the key routes from the Persian Gulf to Singapore and from West Coast India to Singapore indicates that increasingly more concluded spot fixtures were based on 60kt cargo sizes. Over 2019-2025, on the Persian Gulfto-Singapore route, 77 observed spot fixtures were concluded on 60kt cargoes in the LR1 class of clean tankers. On the West Coast India-to-Singapore route, 223 spot fixtures were concluded for 60kt cargoes over the same period.
LR1 vessels typically load between 55,000 mt and 65,000 mt of clean product, including naphtha, gasoline, jet fuel, and gasoil. Platts has observed LR1 vessels frequently being fixed on a spot basis on the Persian Gulf-Singapore route using 60kt stems, particularly for shipments of jet fuel and gasoil.

The new assessment reflects a 60kt clean product cargo loading from the Persian Gulf and discharging in Singapore, using modern LR1 tonnage with an age below 15 years. Platts will assess the route based on observed fixtures, as well as vessels going on subjects, bids/offers, and freight indications obtained through market survey of shipowners, charterers, and shipbrokers.
The assessment is published on a Worldscale percentage basis, together with a corresponding price in $/mt. The $/mt figure is derived from a flat rate based on a basket of key load and discharge ports.
The route basket consists of: Jubail-Singapore Ruwais-Singapore Ras Tanura-Singapore The basket flat rate used to calculate the $/mt freight will be based on the average of the Worldscale flat rates for the three routes. Platts will continue to assess its existing suite of benchmark Clean Persian Gulf-Japan 55kt LR1 assessments. The new assessments appear in Clean Tankerwire, Monthly Clean Tankerwire, Platts Shipping Alert pages 330 and 331, and Platts Tanker Alert pages 2922, and 2923. Assessments follow a 16:30 Singapore timestamp and the Singapore holiday calendar. Please send any feedback or questions to shipping@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts launches daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has launched daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts has launched two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Platts amends freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has amended the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts is using the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), launched Aug. 3, to calculate the outright prices of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change better reflects shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah and Oman from Mina Al Fahal outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Previously, the freight calculation was based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026, followed by a decision on Oct. 15, 2026.
The freight calculation continues to use the metric ton-tobarrel conversion factors applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts is also using the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East Worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices (MPP).
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This was previously calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805,

2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the following price database codes:

Platts to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent (ADB) Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of each month, effective Dec. 1, 2026.
The change follows a review of historical data sets and will align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of the ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward assessment of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
Platts proposed this change in a subscriber note published Sept. 21, 2026.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under the following price database codes:

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
